Through the aspiration and the rest conjoined with restlessness, insight's own class is caused to dwell in defilement, yet this is not able to make insight entirely vanish. The aspiration and the rest conjoined with the agitation of sublime desires, causing insight's own class to dwell in defilement, is also able to make every insight entirely vanish.

Chinese Source

由掉舉俱行欲等，於毗鉢舍那品令住雜染，而不能令毗鉢舍那一切滅沒。妙欲散動俱行欲等，於毗鉢舍那品令住雜染，亦令一切毗鉢舍那皆悉滅沒。

Ru-Yi Translation

Through the aspiration and the rest conjoined with restlessness, insight's own class is caused to dwell in defilement, yet this is not able to make insight entirely vanish. The aspiration and the rest conjoined with the agitation of sublime desires, causing insight's own class to dwell in defilement, is also able to make every insight entirely vanish.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, toward insight (辰二於毗鉢舍那) closes entirely, closing distinguishing the differences (卯二料簡2: 於奢摩他/於毗鉢舍那) entirely, closing **the first two marks (寅一初二相2: 出體/料簡) entirely** — restlessness merely defiles insight without destroying it outright, but agitation toward the five sense-pleasures — severe enough to draw the practitioner right off the cushion in outward pursuit — extinguishes insight entirely, exactly paralleling calm-abiding's own two-tier structure above.

63. Section: The Latter Three Marks Begin — Insight's Own Class of Object

Insight's own class of object means the perception of before and after. The distinctions of this perception should be known in its marks as in the Śrāvaka Ground.

Chinese Source

毗鉢舍那品所緣境者，謂前後想。此想分別，如聲聞地應知其相。

Ru-Yi Translation

Insight's own class of object means the perception of before and after. The distinctions of this perception should be known in its marks as in the Śrāvaka Ground.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Śrāvaka Ground, fascicle 28

This opens the latter three marks (寅二後三相3), the second of separately explaining the marks' two matters, beginning with insight's own class of object (卯一毗鉢舍那品所緣境) — the perception of before and after observes past or future conditioned formations from the vantage of present attention: "dwelling observes sitting" (present attention already arisen observing what is about to arise), "sitting observes lying down" (present attention observing what has already passed), and observing the immediately preceding moment of mind without any gap — detailed further, per Master Cháng Bǎi, in the Śrāvaka Ground, fascicle 28.

64. Section: Calm-Abiding's Own Class of Object

Calm-abiding's own class of object means the perception of above and below. This, too, should be known in its marks as before.

Chinese Source

奢摩他品所緣境者，謂上下想。此亦如前應知其相。

Ru-Yi Translation

Calm-abiding's own class of object means the perception of above and below. This, too, should be known in its marks as before.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Śrāvaka Ground, fascicle 28

This opens calm-abiding's own class of object (卯二奢摩他品所緣境), the second — the perception of above and below observes the body, from crown to sole, as filled throughout with every kind of impure substance (hair, body hair, nails, teeth, and the rest), the very contemplation of the body's own impurity that calm-abiding rests upon.

65. Section: The Object Shared by Both Classes, Closing "The Latter Three Marks" and "Separately Explaining the Marks" Entirely

The object shared by both classes means the perception of light. Toward what belongs to both classes, because of agitation, there is a cultivation of the mind conjoined with various images of light.

Chinese Source

俱品所緣境者，謂光明想。彼於俱品由動搖故，有諸光影俱行心修。

Ru-Yi Translation

The object shared by both classes means the perception of light. Toward what belongs to both classes, because of agitation, there is a cultivation of the mind conjoined with various images of light.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Śrāvaka Ground, fascicle 28

With this, the object shared by both classes (卯三俱品所緣境) closes entirely, closing the latter three marks (寅二後三相3: 毗鉢舍那品所緣境/奢摩他品所緣境/俱品所緣境) entirely, closing **separately explaining the marks (丑二別釋相2: 初二相/後三相) entirely** — the perception of light (whether the light that remedies darkness, such as sunlight, the body's own inward radiance, or the dharma's own light) accompanies the mind throughout both calm-abiding and insight alike, keeping either from being disturbed by torpor, sleepiness, or restlessness.

66. Section: Explaining the Difficult Point

Further, it is not that the aspiration and the rest is conjoined with laziness only in the sense of being conjoined with some further, separate laziness — this is called being conjoined with laziness; diligence, too, in just this way, can carry the meaning of being conjoined with laziness. That is, when diligence itself falls into slackness and slowness, and does not rightly apply itself to sustained, diligent effort, this is called being conjoined with laziness.

Chinese Source

又非如欲等與餘懈怠相應，說名懈怠俱行；精進亦爾，得有懈怠共相應義。然即精進墮在慢緩，不正發勤精進相續，說名懈怠俱行。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, it is not that the aspiration and the rest is conjoined with laziness only in the sense of being conjoined with some further, separate laziness — this is called being conjoined with laziness; diligence, too, in just this way, can carry the meaning of being conjoined with laziness. That is, when diligence itself falls into slackness and slowness, and does not rightly apply itself to sustained, diligent effort, this is called being conjoined with laziness.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the 披尋記

This opens explaining the difficult point (丑三隨難釋), the third of cultivation's four matters — even genuine diligence, if it slackens from its own former pace (practicing eight hours a day, say, and later only six), still counts as being "conjoined with laziness" in this more subtle sense. The 披尋記 clarifies that the "further" laziness named among the eight secondary afflictions (torpor, restlessness, faithlessness, laziness, heedlessness, forgetfulness, distraction, and incorrect knowledge) is being distinguished here from this different, relative sense of laziness — diligence merely slowed, not diligence entirely absent.

67. Section: The Concluding Statement Gathering Them Together, Closing "Cultivation" Entirely

Further, these five marks, one should know, entirely embrace every kind of cultivation. For one who delights in equipoise, through this, equipoise is swiftly brought to completion.

Chinese Source

又此五相，當知總攝一切種修。樂等持者，由此等持速得成滿。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, these five marks, one should know, entirely embrace every kind of cultivation. For one who delights in equipoise, through this, equipoise is swiftly brought to completion.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing a Dharmapada companion story

With this, the concluding statement gathering them together (丑四結總攝) closes entirely, closing **cultivation (子三修4: 略標列/別釋相/隨難釋/結總攝), the third of the embrace of right effort and the bases of supernormal power's five sections, entirely** — these five marks together embrace every kind of cultivation belonging to the bases of supernormal power, and mastering them brings equipoise swiftly to full completion. Master Cháng Bǎi recounts a Dharmapada story of forest-dwelling monks who, mistaking their newly attained concentration for arhatship, are sent by the Buddha to a charnel ground before being received back — there, calmly viewing decayed corpses at first, they are startled to find desire still stirring at the sight of a still-fresh one, and, illuminated by the Buddha's own light and gentle rebuke, recognize they are not yet free of craving and press on with impurity- and selflessness-contemplation until they finally attain liberation — the moral being that only honest, ongoing self-examination, never premature self-congratulation, uncovers a defilement still lying hidden.

68. Section: Equipoise and the Alternate Approach Begin — Setting Out the Distinctions Begins — General Marking

Further, toward the five places of liberation, in accordance with what is fitting, one should know the four heightened samādhis of aspiration and the rest.

Chinese Source

復次，於五解脫處，如其所應，當知欲等增上四種三摩地。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, toward the five places of liberation, in accordance with what is fitting, one should know the four heightened samādhis of aspiration and the rest.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Compendium of Categories, fascicle 13, and the 披尋記

This opens equipoise and the alternate approach (子四等持異門2), the fourth of the embrace of right effort and the bases of supernormal power's five sections, named by the verse's first phrase's fourth word and second phrase's first word "equipoise...the alternate approach" (勇力修等持　異門神通後), explaining the four samādhis through a different lens, beginning with setting out the distinctions (丑一出差別2), beginning with general marking (寅一總標) — five places yield liberation: hearing the true dharma with earnest reverence; expounding it to others; reciting it with sublime intonation; skillfully grasping and reflecting on an excellent samādhi-sign; and, having heard the dharma, dwelling alone in seclusion to reflect, weigh, and carefully observe it — the first three corresponding to hearing-wisdom, the fourth to thinking-wisdom, and the fifth to cultivation-wisdom, detailed further, per Master Cháng Bǎi, in the Compendium of Categories, fascicle 13. Each of the five follows the same pattern: right understanding of the dharma and its meaning gives rise to delight, delight to joy, joy to bodily ease, ease to bliss, bliss to concentration, concentration to true knowing and seeing, true knowing and seeing to weariness, weariness to detachment, and detachment to liberation.

69. Section: Separately Distinguishing Begins — The First Meaning Begins — The Heightened Samādhi of Aspiration

If there is a monk who, relying on pure aspiration and fierce desire, in order to realize the most supreme penetrating wisdom, hears the true dharma from the various tathāgatas and the Buddha's disciples with earnest, respectful reverence — from this hearing, without interruption, by stages, realizes supreme samādhi — one should know this is called the heightened samādhi of aspiration.

Chinese Source

若有苾芻，依淨意樂及猛利欲，為欲證得最勝通慧，從諸如來及佛弟子，殷重恭敬聽聞正法。從聞無間漸次證得勝三摩地。當知是名欲增上三摩地。

Ru-Yi Translation

If there is a monk who, relying on pure aspiration and fierce desire, in order to realize the most supreme penetrating wisdom, hears the true dharma from the various tathāgatas and the Buddha's disciples with earnest, respectful reverence — from this hearing, without interruption, by stages, realizes supreme samādhi — one should know this is called the heightened samādhi of aspiration.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens separately distinguishing (寅二別辨2), beginning with the first meaning (卯一第一義4), a fourfold structure correlating each samādhi to the corresponding place of liberation, beginning with the heightened samādhi of aspiration (辰一欲增上三摩地) — earnestly hearing the true dharma from a buddha or noble disciple, sustained by pure aspiration and fierce longing for supreme wisdom, gradually, without interruption, realizes supreme samādhi — matching the first place of liberation.

70. Section: The Heightened Samādhi of Diligence

There is, further, a monk who, in accordance with the dharma heard and the dharma attained, exerts great effort, gives rise to great diligence, whether rightly expounding and teaching it to others, or reciting it with sublime, excellent intonation — from this, without interruption, by stages of cause and condition, is able to accordingly attain supreme samādhi. One should know this is called the heightened samādhi of diligence.

Chinese Source

復有苾芻，如所聞法、如所得法，起大功用，發大精進，或正為他宣說開示，或以勝妙音詞讀誦。從此無間漸次因緣，能隨獲得勝三摩地。當知是名精進增上三摩地。

Ru-Yi Translation

There is, further, a monk who, in accordance with the dharma heard and the dharma attained, exerts great effort, gives rise to great diligence, whether rightly expounding and teaching it to others, or reciting it with sublime, excellent intonation — from this, without interruption, by stages of cause and condition, is able to accordingly attain supreme samādhi. One should know this is called the heightened samādhi of diligence.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing Great Master Zhìzhě and Great Master Tàixū

This opens the heightened samādhi of diligence (辰二精進增上三摩地), the second — expounding what has been heard to others, or reciting it aloud with sublime intonation, sustains without interruption and yields supreme samādhi in turn, matching the second and third places of liberation. Master Cháng Bǎi cites two later masters exemplifying just this: Great Master Zhìzhě, who, while reciting the Lotus Sūtra's chapter on the former deeds of Medicine King Bodhisattva, entered a still absorption and directly beheld the still-unbroken assembly of Vulture Peak, attaining the initial dhāraṇī and unobstructed eloquence in teaching ever after; and Great Master Tàixū, who, while reading the Great Perfection of Wisdom Sūtra, was suddenly overcome with bodily and mental clarity resembling samādhi, its focus persisting three full days, after which he thoroughly penetrated the doctrines of both emptiness and the true, permanent mind (as well as the illusory-yet-consciousness-only view), going on to champion a broad reform of Chinese Buddhism.

71. Section: The Heightened Samādhi of Mind

There is, further, a monk who, toward the various excellent samādhi-signs, skillfully grasps and reflects upon them, contemplating the bluish, festering corpse and the rest, up to the skeleton as its final limit. Through this object, supreme samādhi arises in sequence. One should know this is called the heightened samādhi of mind.

Chinese Source

復有苾芻，於諸賢善三摩地相善取思惟，觀青瘀等，乃至骨鎖以為邊際。由此所緣，次第生起勝三摩地。當知是名心增上三摩地。

Ru-Yi Translation

There is, further, a monk who, toward the various excellent samādhi-signs, skillfully grasps and reflects upon them, contemplating the bluish, festering corpse and the rest, up to the skeleton as its final limit. Through this object, supreme samādhi arises in sequence. One should know this is called the heightened samādhi of mind.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the heightened samādhi of mind (辰三心增上三摩地), the third — skillfully grasping the sign of impurity-contemplation, from a corpse's bluish discoloration through its bloating, decay, and final reduction to a bare skeleton, sustained reflection on this object yields supreme samādhi, matching the fourth place of liberation.

72. Section: The Heightened Samādhi of Investigation, Closing "The First Meaning" Entirely

There is, further, a monk who, in accordance with the dharma heard and the dharma attained, dwells alone in a secluded place, reflecting, weighing, and carefully, truly observing. Through this cause and condition, supreme samādhi arises by stages. One should know this is called the heightened samādhi of investigation.

Chinese Source

復有苾芻，如所聞法、如所得法，獨處空閑，思惟、籌量、審諦觀察。由此因緣，漸次生起勝三摩地。當知是名觀增上三摩地。

Ru-Yi Translation

There is, further, a monk who, in accordance with the dharma heard and the dharma attained, dwells alone in a secluded place, reflecting, weighing, and carefully, truly observing. Through this cause and condition, supreme samādhi arises by stages. One should know this is called the heightened samādhi of investigation.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, the heightened samādhi of investigation (辰四觀增上三摩地) closes entirely, closing **the first meaning (卯一第一義4: 欲/精進/心/觀增上三摩地), the first of separately distinguishing's two matters, entirely** — dwelling alone in seclusion, reflecting on and weighing the dharma heard and attained through practice in accordance with the dharma, supreme samādhi arises by stages, matching the fifth and final place of liberation, completing the correlation of all four samādhis to their respective places of liberation.

73. Section: The Second Meaning Begins — Generally Marking and Listing

There is, further, a distinction: samādhi arises through four gateways. First, as before, the gateway of a fierce, delighted wish to hear the true dharma, arising from another. Second, the gateway of right instruction: having received unmistaken instruction and exhortation from another, one gives rise, without interruption and with earnest gravity, to preparatory practice; not yet having entered the fundamental, supreme samādhi, one aspires to enter through the gateway of right instruction. Third, the gateway of the mind's delight: having already entered the fundamental, supreme samādhi, in order to turn and attain the remaining, higher supreme samādhi, one proceeds through the gateway of the mind's delight. Fourth, the gateway of equal observation: through extensive learning and retention, one is oneself able, toward the dharma, to observe in accordance with principle through the gateway of equal observation.

Chinese Source

復有差別，謂由四門起三摩地。一、由如前從他生起猛利樂欲聞正法門。二、由從他獲得無倒教授教誡，無間、殷重發起加行，未入根本勝三摩地，為欲趣入正教授門。三、由已入根本勝三摩地，為欲轉得所餘上位勝三摩地，心喜樂門。四、由多聞聞持，自能於法如理觀察平等觀門。

Ru-Yi Translation

There is, further, a distinction: samādhi arises through four gateways. First, as before, the gateway of a fierce, delighted wish to hear the true dharma, arising from another. Second, the gateway of right instruction: having received unmistaken instruction and exhortation from another, one gives rise, without interruption and with earnest gravity, to preparatory practice; not yet having entered the fundamental, supreme samādhi, one aspires to enter through the gateway of right instruction. Third, the gateway of the mind's delight: having already entered the fundamental, supreme samādhi, in order to turn and attain the remaining, higher supreme samādhi, one proceeds through the gateway of the mind's delight. Fourth, the gateway of equal observation: through extensive learning and retention, one is oneself able, toward the dharma, to observe in accordance with principle through the gateway of equal observation.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the second meaning (卯二第二義2), the second of separately distinguishing's two matters, beginning with generally marking and listing (辰一總標列) — a second, complementary lens views the same four samādhis through four gateways: the gateway of hearing (matching aspiration's own samādhi as before), the gateway of right instruction (receiving unmistaken guidance and applying diligent preparatory practice before entering fundamental samādhi, matching diligence's samādhi), the gateway of the mind's delight (advancing from a fundamental samādhi already attained toward a still higher one, matching mind's samādhi), and the gateway of equal observation (a dharma-practitioner's own extensive learning turned toward equal, principled observation of dependent origination, matching investigation's samādhi). Reading continues past this point to confirm what follows — separately correlating this second lens's own four gateways (辰二別配屬) — before further closure is claimed.

74. Section: Separately Correlating, Closing "The Second Meaning," "Separately Distinguishing," and "Setting Out the Distinctions" Entirely

One should know that, here, through the first gateway, the heightened samādhi of aspiration arises; through the second gateway, the heightened samādhi of diligence arises; through the third gateway, the heightened samādhi of mind arises; through the fourth gateway, the heightened samādhi of investigation arises.

Chinese Source

當知此中，由第一門起欲增上三摩地，由第二門起精進增上三摩地，由第三門起心增上三摩地，由第四門起觀增上三摩地。

Ru-Yi Translation

One should know that, here, through the first gateway, the heightened samādhi of aspiration arises; through the second gateway, the heightened samādhi of diligence arises; through the third gateway, the heightened samādhi of mind arises; through the fourth gateway, the heightened samādhi of investigation arises.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, separately correlating (辰二別配屬) closes entirely, closing the second meaning (卯二第二義2: 總標列/別配屬) entirely, closing **separately distinguishing (寅二別辨2: 第一義/第二義) entirely**, closing **setting out the distinctions (丑一出差別2: 總標/別辨), the first of equipoise and the alternate approach's two matters, entirely** — the four gateways (hearing, right instruction, the mind's delight, equal observation) map exactly onto the four heightened samādhis (aspiration, diligence, mind, investigation) in the same order, completing this second, complementary correlation.

75. Section: Pointing to the Remaining Marks, Closing "Equipoise and the Alternate Approach" Entirely

The remaining distinctions of meaning, and the distinctions of the conduct of severance, should be known in their marks as in the Śrāvaka Ground.

Chinese Source

所餘分別義及分別斷行，如聲聞地應知其相。

Ru-Yi Translation

The remaining distinctions of meaning, and the distinctions of the conduct of severance, should be known in their marks as in the Śrāvaka Ground.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Śrāvaka Ground, fascicle 29

With this, pointing to the remaining marks (丑二指餘相) closes entirely, closing **equipoise and the alternate approach (子四等持異門2: 出差別/指餘相), the fourth of the embrace of right effort and the bases of supernormal power's five sections, entirely** — the Śrāvaka Ground, fascicle 29, is cited for the fuller distinction of the four named samādhis (aspiration's, diligence's, mind's, investigation's) and the eight-part conduct of severance that perfects them once affliction's manifest activity, though suppressed, still leaves its seed unsevered: aspiration (the wish for samādhi's own completion), exertion (unflagging, resolute preparatory effort), faith (undoubting confidence that sustained practice will succeed), ease (joy born of that confidence, itself fueling further diligence), mindfulness (the nine progressive stages of settling the mind), right knowledge (insight's own wisdom, alert to whether mindfulness has lapsed or wholesome and unwholesome states are present), volition (the mental factor that actively assembles the supporting conditions — precepts, sense-restraint, wakeful yoga, moderation in eating — for calm-abiding and insight), and equanimity (a mind undisturbed by craving, aversion, torpor, restlessness, or doubt toward any object across the three times) — together known as the eight excellent conducts, each supporting the four samādhis toward their completion.

76. Section: Supernormal Power Begins — Generally Marking and Distinguishing

Further, cultivating the various bases of supernormal power as a support, one is able to rightly bring forth the various noble supernormal powers. There is no outside path that, cultivating the various bases of supernormal power, is able to rightly bring forth the various noble supernormal powers.

Chinese Source

復次，修諸神足以為依止，能正引發諸聖神通。無有外道修諸神足能正引發諸聖神通。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, cultivating the various bases of supernormal power as a support, one is able to rightly bring forth the various noble supernormal powers. There is no outside path that, cultivating the various bases of supernormal power, is able to rightly bring forth the various noble supernormal powers.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens supernormal power (子五神通2), the fifth and final of the embrace of right effort and the bases of supernormal power's five sections, named by the verse's second phrase's second half "supernormal power, coming last" (異門神通後), beginning with generally marking and distinguishing (丑一總標簡) — the Buddhadharma's own noble supernormal powers, built on the four bases of supernormal power, surpass whatever powers outside paths may possess, since only the noble ones' own attainment counts as genuinely "noble" supernormal power.

77. Section: Separately Explaining the Marks Begins — Raising the Most Supreme

Further, the various noble ones, bringing forth every kind of most supreme supernormal power, are able, in accordance with their own aspiration and delight, to extend their life-formations, dwelling for a single eon, or beyond a single eon — that is, exceeding one eon. The person of indeterminate lineage is called "of that class"; one should know that this class exists only within the inner dharma.

Chinese Source

又諸聖者引發所有最勝神通，隨所願樂，延諸壽行，或住一劫，或一劫餘，謂過一劫。不定種姓補特伽羅名為物類，當知此類唯住內法。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, the various noble ones, bringing forth every kind of most supreme supernormal power, are able, in accordance with their own aspiration and delight, to extend their life-formations, dwelling for a single eon, or beyond a single eon — that is, exceeding one eon. The person of indeterminate lineage is called "of that class"; one should know that this class exists only within the inner dharma.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the 瑜伽論記 and the decisive section on the Bodhisattva Ground, fascicle 80, together with the 披尋記

This opens separately explaining the marks (丑二別釋相2), beginning with raising the most supreme (寅一舉最勝) — a noble one who has mastered the four bases of supernormal power can, as desired, extend their own lifespan by a full eon or more; the 瑜伽論記 identifies four kinds of supernormal power at play here: transformation, transformation of nature, sustaining across time, and transformation of time. This life-extension applies specifically to the śrāvaka of indeterminate lineage — one who will, in time, turn from the lesser vehicle toward supreme enlightenment — rather than to a śrāvaka of fixed lineage bound only for final nirvāṇa without remainder; the decisive section on the Bodhisattva Ground, fascicle 80, per the 披尋記, confirms the Buddha spoke of this "class" (物類) with such śrāvaka-turning-bodhisattva practitioners specifically in mind.

78. Section: Distinguishing the Powerless Begins — Toward Four Matters

Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of transformation, toward four matters, is unable to transform them: first, the faculties; second, the mind; third, the mental factors; fourth, karma and karma's ripening.

Chinese Source

又諸聖者變化神通，於其四事不能變化。一者、根，二者、心，三者、心所有法，四者、業及業異熟。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of transformation, toward four matters, is unable to transform them: first, the faculties; second, the mind; third, the mental factors; fourth, karma and karma's ripening.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens distinguishing the powerless (寅二簡無能3), the second of separately explaining the marks' two matters, a threefold structure, beginning with toward four matters (卯一於四事) — however extraordinary a noble one's supernormal power may be, it cannot alter a being's own faculties, cannot conjure a mind or its mental factors out of nothing, and cannot transform karma or its ripening — four boundaries even the greatest supernormal power never crosses.

79. Section: Toward Transformation of Nature Begins — Transforming the Feeling Received Begins — Raising Pleasant Feeling Set Against Painful Feeling

Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of transforming nature is unable to transform karma conducive to pleasant feeling, causing its own nature to change into karma conducive to painful feeling.

Chinese Source

又諸聖者變性神通，不能轉變順樂受業，令自性改成順苦受。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of transforming nature is unable to transform karma conducive to pleasant feeling, causing its own nature to change into karma conducive to painful feeling.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens toward transformation of nature (卯二於變性2), the second of distinguishing the powerless's three matters, beginning with transforming the feeling received (辰一轉令改受2), beginning with raising pleasant feeling set against painful feeling (巳一舉樂受望苦受) — karma already conducive to a pleasant ripening cannot be transformed, even by supernormal power, into karma conducive to a painful one; wholesome karma already accumulated can never be forced to ripen as if it were unwholesome.

80. Section: The Parallel Case of Painful Feeling Set Against Pleasant Feeling, Closing "Transforming the Feeling Received" Entirely

Just as karma conducive to pleasant feeling cannot become karma conducive to painful feeling, so too, karma conducive to painful feeling toward karma conducive to pleasant feeling — one should know that it is likewise so.

Chinese Source

如順樂受望順苦受，順苦受業望順樂受，應知亦爾。

Ru-Yi Translation

Just as karma conducive to pleasant feeling cannot become karma conducive to painful feeling, so too, karma conducive to painful feeling toward karma conducive to pleasant feeling — one should know that it is likewise so.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, the parallel case of painful feeling set against pleasant feeling (巳二例苦受望樂受) closes entirely, closing transforming the feeling received (辰一轉令改受2: 舉樂受望苦受/例苦受望樂受) entirely — the same holds in reverse: unwholesome karma already fixed toward a painful ripening can never be transformed by supernormal power into karma conducive to pleasure.

81. Section: Transforming Toward No Feeling at All Begins — Raising Feeling Conducive to Neither Pain Nor Pleasure

If karma is able to be conducive to neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling, one should know that it is, in the end, conducive to neither pain nor pleasure. Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of sustaining is unable to sustain karma conducive to neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling in such a way as to make it produce no feeling at all.

Chinese Source

若業能順非苦樂受，當知畢竟順非苦樂。又諸聖者住持神通，不能住持順非苦樂受業，令成無受。

Ru-Yi Translation

If karma is able to be conducive to neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling, one should know that it is, in the end, conducive to neither pain nor pleasure. Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of sustaining is unable to sustain karma conducive to neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling in such a way as to make it produce no feeling at all.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens transforming toward no feeling at all (辰二轉令無受2), the second of toward transformation of nature's two matters, beginning with raising feeling conducive to neither pain nor pleasure (巳一舉順非苦樂受) — karma already fixed toward neutral feeling remains fixed toward neutral feeling; supernormal power cannot even suppress karma's ripening into producing no feeling whatsoever — karma, once set, will always produce some feeling.

82. Section: The Parallel Case of the Remaining Pleasant and Painful Feelings, Closing "Transforming Toward No Feeling at All" and "Toward Transformation of Nature" Entirely

The rest is likewise so.

Chinese Source

餘亦如是。

Ru-Yi Translation

The rest is likewise so.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the 披尋記

With this, the parallel case of the remaining pleasant and painful feelings (巳二例餘苦樂受) closes entirely, closing transforming toward no feeling at all (辰二轉令無受2: 舉順非苦樂受/例餘苦樂受) entirely, closing **toward transformation of nature (卯二於變性2: 轉令改受/轉令無受), the second of distinguishing the powerless's three matters, entirely** — per the 披尋記, "the rest" names karma conducive to pleasant and painful feeling, likewise unable to be sustained into producing no feeling at all; every karma leaves its trace, and no noble one's supernormal power, however great, can erase that trace entirely.

83. Section: Toward Transformation of Time, Closing "Distinguishing the Powerless," "Separately Explaining the Marks," "Supernormal Power," and the Entire Embrace of Right Effort and the Bases of Supernormal Power Entirely

Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of transforming time is unable to transform karma conducive to being felt in the present dharma into karma conducive to being felt in a later dharma; nor karma conducive to being felt in a later dharma into karma conducive to being felt in the present dharma.

Chinese Source

又諸聖者變時神通，不能轉變順現法受業，令成順後法受業；及順後法受業，令成順現法受業。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, the various noble ones' supernormal power of transforming time is unable to transform karma conducive to being felt in the present dharma into karma conducive to being felt in a later dharma; nor karma conducive to being felt in a later dharma into karma conducive to being felt in the present dharma.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, toward transformation of time (卯三於變時) closes entirely, closing **distinguishing the powerless (寅二簡無能3: 於四事/於變性/於變時) entirely**, closing **separately explaining the marks (丑二別釋相2: 舉最勝/簡無能) entirely**, closing **supernormal power (子五神通2: 總標簡/別釋相), the fifth and final of the embrace of right effort and the bases of supernormal power's five sections, entirely** — supernormal power cannot even shift when karma ripens, whether pulling a future fruit into the present or pushing a present fruit into the future; karma's own timing, like its nature and its nonexistence, lies entirely beyond even a noble one's own supernormal reach — the common saying that "supernormal power cannot overcome karma" (神通不敵業力) captures exactly this teaching. **With this section's closure, the embrace of right effort and the bases of supernormal power (壬二正斷神足攝2: 勇/力/修/等持異門/神通), the second of the factors of enlightenment's nine great topics, closes in its complete entirety.**

84. Section: The Embrace of the Faculties Begins — The Verse Marking and Listing

Further, a summary verse says: establishing them, and the sphere of their operation; the faculty of wisdom is supreme; one should know, afterward, the abidings — the outsider, the ordinary being's own class, and the rest.

Chinese Source

復次，嗢柁南曰：

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, a summary verse says:

Chinese Source

安立所行境　慧根為最勝　當知後安住　外異生品等

Ru-Yi Translation

Establishing them, and the sphere of their operation; the faculty of wisdom is supreme; one should know, afterward, the abidings — the outsider, the ordinary being's own class, and the rest.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens **the embrace of the faculties (壬三根攝2), the third of the factors of enlightenment's nine great topics**, named by the general summary verse's second line's second half "the faculties" (神足及根力) — treating the five faculties (信根/進根/念根/定根/慧根) together with the seventeen further faculties that, with them, make up the traditional twenty-two — beginning with the verse marking and listing (癸一頌標列) — four matters unfold in the long-form explanation: establishing the twenty-two faculties, the sphere in which they properly operate, the faculty of wisdom's own supremacy among them, and, afterward, the abidings distinguishing the outsider, the ordinary being's own class within the inner dharma, and the remaining classes of practitioner.

85. Section: The Long-Form Explanation Begins — Establishing Them Begins — Marking and Listing

Briefly, through the increasing meaning of six places, one should know that the twenty-two faculties are established. What are the six? First, the increasing meaning of grasping an object; second, the increasing meaning of continuing the family lineage; third, the increasing meaning of the causes and conditions of sustaining life, and of each distinct undertaking's preparatory exertion; fourth, the increasing meaning of experiencing the desirable and undesirable fruit produced by former lives' various karmas, and of creating new karma; fifth, the increasing meaning of tending toward mundane freedom from desire; sixth, the increasing meaning of tending toward supramundane freedom from desire.

Chinese Source

略由六處增上義故，當知建立二十二根。何等為六？一、能取境界增上義故；二、繼嗣家族增上義故；三、活命因緣各別事業加行士用增上義故；四、受用先世諸業所作愛不愛果及造新業增上義故；五、趣向世間離欲增上義故；六、趣向出世離欲增上義故。

Ru-Yi Translation

Briefly, through the increasing meaning of six places, one should know that the twenty-two faculties are established. What are the six? First, the increasing meaning of grasping an object; second, the increasing meaning of continuing the family lineage; third, the increasing meaning of the causes and conditions of sustaining life, and of each distinct undertaking's preparatory exertion; fourth, the increasing meaning of experiencing the desirable and undesirable fruit produced by former lives' various karmas, and of creating new karma; fifth, the increasing meaning of tending toward mundane freedom from desire; sixth, the increasing meaning of tending toward supramundane freedom from desire.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the long-form explanation (癸二長行釋4), a fourfold structure matching the verse's four matters, beginning with establishing them (子一安立3), named by the verse's first phrase's first half "establishing them" (安立所行境), beginning with marking and listing (丑一標列) — six increasing meanings ground the establishment of the twenty-two faculties: grasping an object, continuing the family lineage, sustaining life and its undertakings, experiencing karma's fruit while creating new karma, tending toward mundane freedom from desire, and tending toward supramundane freedom from desire.

86. Section: Correlating and Explaining Begins — The Eye and the Other Five Faculties

One should know that, here, with the eye faculty first and the mind faculty last, these six faculties have an increasing meaning in grasping their object.

Chinese Source

當知此中，眼根最初，意根為後，如是六根，於取境界有增上義。

Ru-Yi Translation

One should know that, here, with the eye faculty first and the mind faculty last, these six faculties have an increasing meaning in grasping their object.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens correlating and explaining (丑二配釋6), a sixfold structure matching the six increasing meanings, beginning with the eye and the other five faculties (寅一眼等六根) — the six sense-faculties, eye through mind, together fulfill the first increasing meaning: grasping the six kinds of objects (form, sound, smell, taste, touch, and dharmas).

87. Section: The Male and Female Faculties

The two faculties, male and female, have an increasing meaning in being able to continue the family lineage's descendants.

Chinese Source

男女二根，於能繼嗣家族子孫有增上義。

Ru-Yi Translation

The two faculties, male and female, have an increasing meaning in being able to continue the family lineage's descendants.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the male and female faculties (寅二男女二根), the second — these two faculties fulfill the second increasing meaning, sustaining human procreation and family continuity.

88. Section: The Life Faculty

The single life faculty has an increasing meaning, for those who cherish life, in the causes and conditions of sustaining life, and in each distinct undertaking's preparatory exertion.

Chinese Source

命根一種，於愛命者活命因緣各別事業加行士用有增上義。

Ru-Yi Translation

The single life faculty has an increasing meaning, for those who cherish life, in the causes and conditions of sustaining life, and in each distinct undertaking's preparatory exertion.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the life faculty (寅三命根), the third — the single life faculty fulfills the third increasing meaning, sustaining the very continuity of life that makes any undertaking, worldly or spiritual, possible at all.

89. Section: Pleasure and the Other Four Feeling-Faculties

With pleasure first and equanimity last, these five faculties have an increasing meaning in experiencing the desirable and undesirable fruit produced by former karma, and in creating new karma.

Chinese Source

樂最為初，捨為其後，如是五根，於其受用先業所作愛不愛果及造新業有增上義。

Ru-Yi Translation

With pleasure first and equanimity last, these five faculties have an increasing meaning in experiencing the desirable and undesirable fruit produced by former karma, and in creating new karma.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens pleasure and the other four feeling-faculties (寅四樂等五根), the fourth — the five feeling-faculties (pleasure, pain, sorrow, joy, equanimity) fulfill the fourth increasing meaning: experiencing the desirable or undesirable fruit of former karma (pleasant feeling breeding attachment, painful feeling breeding aversion) while, in turn, driving the creation of yet more karma — feeling's own power to perpetuate the endless cycle of rebirth. Reading continues past this point to confirm what follows — the fifth and sixth increasing meanings' own correlation (寅五信等五根, presumably the five faith-through-wisdom faculties, and beyond) — before further closure is claimed.

90. Section: Faith and the Other Four Path-Faculties

With faith first and wisdom last, these five faculties have an increasing meaning in being able to tend toward mundane freedom from desire.

Chinese Source

信為最初，慧為其後，如是五根，於能趣向世間離欲有增上義。

Ru-Yi Translation

With faith first and wisdom last, these five faculties have an increasing meaning in being able to tend toward mundane freedom from desire.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens faith and the other four path-faculties (寅五信等五根), the fifth — the five faculties of faith, diligence, mindfulness, concentration, and wisdom fulfill the fifth increasing meaning, helping the practitioner accomplish mundane meditative freedom from desire.

91. Section: The Three Uncontaminated Faculties, Closing "Correlating and Explaining" Entirely

The three uncontaminated faculties — the faculty of coming to know what was not yet known, the faculty of full knowledge, and the faculty of complete knowledge — have an increasing meaning in being able to tend toward supramundane freedom from desire, to its very utmost finality.

Chinese Source

未知當知、已知、具知三無漏根，於能趣向出世離欲最極究竟有增上義。

Ru-Yi Translation

The three uncontaminated faculties — the faculty of coming to know what was not yet known, the faculty of full knowledge, and the faculty of complete knowledge — have an increasing meaning in being able to tend toward supramundane freedom from desire, to its very utmost finality.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, the three uncontaminated faculties (寅六三無漏根) closes entirely, closing **correlating and explaining (丑二配釋6: 眼等六根/男女二根/命根/樂等五根/信等五根/三無漏根) entirely** — each of the three uncontaminated faculties gathers the same nine dharmas (faith, diligence, mindfulness, concentration, wisdom, joy, ease, equanimity, and mind), differing only by depth: the faculty of coming to know what was not yet known belongs to the path toward the first fruit; the faculty of full knowledge spans from the first fruit through the path toward the fourth; and the faculty of complete knowledge belongs to the arhat alone, all nine dharmas perfected — together fulfilling the sixth increasing meaning, tending toward supramundane freedom from desire's own utmost finality.

92. Section: The Concluding Statement Gathering Them Together, Closing "Establishing Them" Entirely

Every meaning that appears within the entire world is, in its full measure, only this much. One should know that what is able to bring this meaning to its ultimate completion never exceeds these twenty-two faculties; therefore every faculty is embraced by these twenty-two.

Chinese Source

一切世間所現見義，其唯此量。當知是義能究竟者，無出於此二十二根，故一切根二十二攝。

Ru-Yi Translation

Every meaning that appears within the entire world is, in its full measure, only this much. One should know that what is able to bring this meaning to its ultimate completion never exceeds these twenty-two faculties; therefore every faculty is embraced by these twenty-two.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing a companion sūtra on the great roof-beam

With this, the concluding statement gathering them together (丑三結攝) closes entirely, closing **establishing them (子一安立3: 標列/配釋/結攝), the first of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, entirely** — every meaning appearing anywhere in the world falls within these twenty-two faculties' own scope, and nothing capable of bringing that meaning to ultimate completion lies outside them. Master Cháng Bǎi cites a companion sūtra likening the five faculties to a hall's construction: many timbers are needed, but only once the great central roof-beam is raised can every lesser beam find its proper support — just so, wisdom, as the foremost among faith, diligence, mindfulness, concentration, and wisdom, is what sustains and holds together every other faculty's own merit.

93. Section: The Sphere of Their Operation Begins — Setting Out the Wrong Discrimination

Further, there is a certain kind that thinks thus: if there were no inner self relying on the six sense-faculty doors to operate within the six kinds of objects, then these six faculties, each operating separately, each within its own separate object, and each faculty able only to receive its own particular sphere of operation — who, then, is able to receive the very nature of what all six faculties, taken together, operate within? One should know that this arises from an inability to penetrate the principle of dependent origination, and so gives rise to a wrong discrimination toward the formations.

Chinese Source

復次，或有一類作是思惟：若無內我託六根門，行六境界，如是六根各別所行、各別境界，然此六根唯能領受自所行境，誰能領受如是六根所行境性？當知此由不能了達緣起道理，故於諸行起邪分別。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, there is a certain kind that thinks thus: if there were no inner self relying on the six sense-faculty doors to operate within the six kinds of objects, then these six faculties, each operating separately, each within its own separate object, and each faculty able only to receive its own particular sphere of operation — who, then, is able to receive the very nature of what all six faculties, taken together, operate within? One should know that this arises from an inability to penetrate the principle of dependent origination, and so gives rise to a wrong discrimination toward the formations.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the sphere of their operation (子二所行境3), the second of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, named by the verse's first phrase's second half "the sphere of their operation" (安立所行境), beginning with setting out the wrong discrimination (丑一出邪分別) — noticing that each of the six sense-faculties operates within its own separate object, someone unfamiliar with dependent origination wrongly concludes that a unifying self must exist to receive what all six faculties, taken together, experience — a wrong discrimination born of not understanding how conditioned phenomena actually cohere without any such self.

94. Section: Manifesting the Principle of Dependent Origination Begins — Distinguishing What Should Be Cultivated Begins — Cultivating the Four Foundations of Mindfulness

As for the principle of dependent origination: it means that, at such a time as the practitioner of yoga, toward the inward six faculties, engages them with principled attention, diligently applying the preparatory practice of cultivating the four foundations of mindfulness — at just that time, these four foundations of mindfulness receive the very nature of what the six faculties operate within. Precisely because of this purity, it is called transcendence.

Chinese Source

緣起理者，謂若有時，修瑜伽師於內六根如理攀緣，精勤加行修四念住；即於爾時，此四念住領受六根所行境性。即此於彼由清淨故，名為出離。

Ru-Yi Translation

As for the principle of dependent origination: it means that, at such a time as the practitioner of yoga, toward the inward six faculties, engages them with principled attention, diligently applying the preparatory practice of cultivating the four foundations of mindfulness — at just that time, these four foundations of mindfulness receive the very nature of what the six faculties operate within. Precisely because of this purity, it is called transcendence.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens manifesting the principle of dependent origination (丑二明緣起理2), the second of the sphere of their operation's three matters, beginning with distinguishing what should be cultivated (寅一辨應修3), a threefold structure, beginning with cultivating the four foundations of mindfulness (卯一修四念住) — no unifying self is needed: when the practitioner brings principled attention to the six faculties through diligently cultivating the four foundations of mindfulness, it is precisely the four foundations themselves that come to receive what the six faculties, together, operate within — and this very purification is what "transcendence" means.

95. Section: Attaining the Seven Factors of Enlightenment

Further, precisely because of diligently cultivating the four foundations of mindfulness, one first penetrates the principle of the truths and attains the seven factors of enlightenment; at just that time, these various factors of enlightenment, being true and being real, receive the very nature of what the foundations of mindfulness operate within.

Chinese Source

又即勤修四念住故，初達諦理得七覺支；即於爾時，此諸覺支真故實故，領受念住所行境性。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, precisely because of diligently cultivating the four foundations of mindfulness, one first penetrates the principle of the truths and attains the seven factors of enlightenment; at just that time, these various factors of enlightenment, being true and being real, receive the very nature of what the foundations of mindfulness operate within.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens attaining the seven factors of enlightenment (卯二得七覺支), the second — diligent cultivation of the four foundations first penetrates the noble truths and yields the seven factors of enlightenment (mindfulness, investigation of dharmas, diligence, joy, pliancy, concentration, and equanimity); these factors, now conjoined with true reality, in turn receive what the four foundations themselves operate within, the sphere of receiving continuing to purify and deepen at every stage.

96. Section: Arising in Clear Liberation, Closing "Distinguishing What Should Be Cultivated" Entirely

Further, through the cause and condition of cultivating the factors of enlightenment, clear liberation arises. At just that time, such clear liberation receives the factors of enlightenment, which have already been well cultivated.

Chinese Source

又由修習覺支因緣，起於明脫。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, through the cause and condition of cultivating the factors of enlightenment, clear liberation arises.

Chinese Source

即於爾時，如是明脫領受覺支已善修習。

Ru-Yi Translation

At just that time, such clear liberation receives the factors of enlightenment, which have already been well cultivated.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the 披尋記

With this, arising in clear liberation (卯三起於明脫) closes entirely, closing **distinguishing what should be cultivated (寅一辨應修3: 修四念住/得七覺支/起於明脫), the first of manifesting the principle of dependent origination's two matters, entirely** — cultivating the seven factors of enlightenment gives rise to clear liberation, the wisdom and vision that frees the mind from ignorance; per the 披尋記, this clear liberation now itself receives the well-cultivated factors of enlightenment as its own sphere, the receiving relationship cascading upward one more step, from faculties to foundations to factors to liberation itself.

97. Section: Manifesting the Finality, Closing "Manifesting the Principle of Dependent Origination" Entirely

From this point on, one no longer needs to cultivate what is operated within; having truly already severed every affliction, at just that time, through the cessation of every affliction, one attains nirvāṇa, free of excessive pride. Precisely because of being free of excessive pride, there is now, in actual fact, ultimate clear liberation, which truly receives the very nature of what the already-attained clear liberation operates within. Because of this transcendence of every conditioned dharma whatsoever, one should know that clear liberation, too, attains transcendence.

Chinese Source

從此已後，不復應修所行境性。如實已斷一切煩惱，即於爾時，於諸煩惱斷滅涅槃，離增上慢。即由遠離增上慢故，此現實有究竟明脫，如實領受已得明脫所行境性。由此出離一切所有有為法故，當知明脫亦得出離。

Ru-Yi Translation

From this point on, one no longer needs to cultivate what is operated within; having truly already severed every affliction, at just that time, through the cessation of every affliction, one attains nirvāṇa, free of excessive pride. Precisely because of being free of excessive pride, there is now, in actual fact, ultimate clear liberation, which truly receives the very nature of what the already-attained clear liberation operates within. Because of this transcendence of every conditioned dharma whatsoever, one should know that clear liberation, too, attains transcendence.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, manifesting the finality (寅二顯究竟) closes entirely, closing **manifesting the principle of dependent origination (丑二明緣起理2: 辨應修/顯究竟), the second of the sphere of their operation's three matters, entirely** — at the arhat's own stage, with every affliction genuinely severed and every trace of excessive pride gone, ultimate clear liberation itself becomes the final sphere received, and, since even this liberation belongs to the conditioned realm's own transcendence, clear liberation is itself, in the end, transcended — no further cultivation, no further object, remains.

98. Section: Distinguishing the Right Designation Begins — Distinguishing What Is Not Nirvāṇa

Within nirvāṇa, neither of the two designations, the grasping subject and the grasped object, exists at all, since every conceptual proliferation is permanently extinguished and departed.

Chinese Source

於涅槃中，能取、所取二種施設皆無所有，一切戲論永滅離故。

Ru-Yi Translation

Within nirvāṇa, neither of the two designations, the grasping subject and the grasped object, exists at all, since every conceptual proliferation is permanently extinguished and departed.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens distinguishing the right designation (丑三辨正施設3), the third of the sphere of their operation's three matters, beginning with distinguishing what is not nirvāṇa (寅一簡非涅槃) — the very subject-object structure this whole section has traced (faculties received by foundations, foundations by factors, factors by liberation) belongs entirely to the conditioned realm; nirvāṇa itself, with every conceptual elaboration extinguished, admits no such designation of a grasping subject or grasped object at all.

99. Section: Explaining the Distinction

Therefore, up through every conditioned dharma, one can, in turn, ask and answer, designating the distinct discourse of the grasping subject and the grasped object; within ultimate nirvāṇa, the unconditioned dharma, every distinct discourse of questioning and answering is entirely without principle.

Chinese Source

是故乃至諸有為法，可得展轉問答施設能取、所取言論差別；究竟涅槃無為法中，一切問答言論差別，皆不如理。

Ru-Yi Translation

Therefore, up through every conditioned dharma, one can, in turn, ask and answer, designating the distinct discourse of the grasping subject and the grasped object; within ultimate nirvāṇa, the unconditioned dharma, every distinct discourse of questioning and answering is entirely without principle.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens explaining the distinction (寅二釋其差別), the second — conditioned dharmas alone admit the back-and-forth of conventional discourse about subject and object; the unconditioned, ultimate nirvāṇa admits no such discourse whatsoever, every question and answer about it being, strictly speaking, beside the point.

100. Section: Concluding "Only Defilement and Purity," Closing "Distinguishing the Right Designation" and "The Sphere of Their Operation" Entirely

Therefore one should know that, within selflessness, one should rightly manifest that there is only defilement, and only purity.

Chinese Source

是故當知，於無我中，應正顯示唯有雜染、唯有清淨。

Ru-Yi Translation

Therefore one should know that, within selflessness, one should rightly manifest that there is only defilement, and only purity.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing a companion sūtra on the three uncontaminated faculties in verse

With this, concluding "only defilement and purity" (寅三結唯染淨) closes entirely, closing distinguishing the right designation (丑三辨正施設3: 簡非涅槃/釋其差別/結唯染淨) entirely, closing **the sphere of their operation (子二所行境3: 出邪分別/明緣起理/辨正施設), the second of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, entirely** — no abiding self is ever found anywhere among the six faculties or their objects; what exists is only conditioned defilement or conditioned purity, arising and passing without any controlling agent behind them. Master Cháng Bǎi traces this whole section's teaching to a companion sūtra in which the Buddha, having taught the three uncontaminated faculties (the faculty of coming to know what was not yet known, spanning the fifteen moments before the path of seeing; the faculty of full knowledge, from the first fruit through the path toward the fourth; and the faculty of complete knowledge, the arhat's own), sums up in verse: the trainee's own non-discriminating wisdom advances in accordance with the straight path already cultivated; diligent skillful means and well-guarded mind lead, in time, to knowing "my births are exhausted," the unobstructed path already known, liberation already known, and, at the very last, complete knowledge itself — freedom from self, right wisdom, an unshakeable, liberated mind, every conditioned thing exhausted, all nine faculties fully perfected, the six faculties resting in the utmost equanimity, the final body sustained by past karma alone, and every host of Māra's afflictions subdued — describing, verse by verse, the arhat's own attainment.

101. Section: Wisdom-Faculty's Supremacy Begins — Distinguishing Begins — What Is Accomplished at the Very Beginning

Further, if there is a person of sharp wisdom, of the kind whose faculties are keen, who, through the power of discernment, gives principled attention and reflects on the various dharmas, then, toward nirvāṇa, attains right faith and understanding.

Chinese Source

復次，若有黠慧、諸根猛利種類士夫補特伽羅，由思擇力，如理作意思惟諸法，乃於涅槃得正信解。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, if there is a person of sharp wisdom, of the kind whose faculties are keen, who, through the power of discernment, gives principled attention and reflects on the various dharmas, then, toward nirvāṇa, attains right faith and understanding.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens wisdom-faculty's supremacy (子三慧根最勝2), the third of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, named by the verse's second phrase "the faculty of wisdom is supreme" (慧根為最勝), beginning with distinguishing (丑一辨2), beginning with what is accomplished at the very beginning (寅一最初所作) — a sharp-witted practitioner's discernment, applied through principled attention to the dharma, is what first produces genuine faith and understanding toward nirvāṇa — wisdom already at work from the very outset of practice.

102. Section: What Is Accomplished Afterward, Closing "Distinguishing" Entirely

Through the increase of this, one gives rise to diligent effort; through this increase, one is able, toward body and the rest's objects, to settle in right mindfulness; through this increase, one is able to bring the mind to one-pointed focus upon its object; through this increase, one truly knows and truly observes every dharma. By this cause and condition, one is able to reach finality.

Chinese Source

由此增上，發勤精進；此增上故，能於身等所緣境界安住正念；此增上故，能於所緣令心一趣；此增上故，於一切法如實了知、如實觀見。由是因緣，能到究竟。

Ru-Yi Translation

Through the increase of this, one gives rise to diligent effort; through this increase, one is able, toward body and the rest's objects, to settle in right mindfulness; through this increase, one is able to bring the mind to one-pointed focus upon its object; through this increase, one truly knows and truly observes every dharma. By this cause and condition, one is able to reach finality.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, what is accomplished afterward (寅二次後所作) closes entirely, closing **distinguishing (丑一辨2: 最初所作/次後所作) entirely** — that same initial wisdom's own increasing power then drives diligent effort, settles right mindfulness upon the four foundations, brings the mind to one-pointed concentration, and finally yields true knowing and seeing of every dharma, carrying the practitioner all the way to ultimate finality — wisdom active at every single stage, from first to last.

103. Section: The Concluding Statement, Closing "Wisdom-Faculty's Supremacy" Entirely

Therefore this wisdom, whether at the very beginning or afterward, has much work to accomplish; therefore it is said that the faculty of wisdom is the most supreme.

Chinese Source

是故此慧，若初若後多有所作，故說慧根最為殊勝。

Ru-Yi Translation

Therefore this wisdom, whether at the very beginning or afterward, has much work to accomplish; therefore it is said that the faculty of wisdom is the most supreme.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing several companion sūtras, including one on bodhicitta's own five faculties

With this, the concluding statement (丑二結) closes entirely, closing **wisdom-faculty's supremacy (子三慧根最勝2: 辨/結), the third of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, entirely** — since wisdom is at work from the very first stirring of faith through the very last stage of realization, it is rightly called the most supreme of all the faculties. Master Cháng Bǎi cites a cluster of companion sūtras: one likening the five faculties to a hall's construction (wisdom as the great roof-beam upholding every other timber); one correlating each faculty to a doctrinal set (faith to the four confirmed purities in the Three Jewels and precepts, diligence to the four right efforts, mindfulness to the four foundations, concentration to the four dhyānas, and wisdom to the four noble truths, wisdom again standing foremost); one teaching that whoever accomplishes the wisdom faculty necessarily accomplishes the remaining four as well, since it is precisely the wisdom that recognizes beginningless ignorance's own grip on saṃsāra that drives every other faculty's own success; and one, carrying a distinctly Mahāyāna resonance, framing all five faculties as arising specifically from a noble disciple's own aspiration toward the Tathāgata's own bodhicitta.

104. Section: The Abidings Begins — The Outsider Ordinary Being's Own Class

Further, if, relying on the various buddhas' unsurpassed enlightenment, the right faith attained, up through right wisdom, does not exist even at the mundane level, one should know that this abides in the class of the outsider ordinary being.

Chinese Source

復次，若依諸佛無上菩提所得正信乃至正慧，於此世間亦無有者，當知此住外異生品。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, if, relying on the various buddhas' unsurpassed enlightenment, the right faith attained, up through right wisdom, does not exist even at the mundane level, one should know that this abides in the class of the outsider ordinary being.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the abidings (子四安住品類3), the fourth and final of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, named by the verse's third phrase's second half and fourth phrase "the abidings — the outsider, the ordinary being's own class, and the rest" (當知後安住　外異生品等), beginning with the outsider ordinary being's own class (丑一外異生品) — a being who has not attained even the mundane five faculties (faith through wisdom) taught through the buddhas' own unsurpassed enlightenment abides entirely outside the inner dharma, in the class of the outsider ordinary being.

105. Section: The Inner Ordinary Being's Own Class

If, toward this very dharma, there is only the mundane, and no supramundane attainment, one should know that this abides in the class of the inner ordinary being, not the outsider ordinary being.

Chinese Source

即於此法唯有世間，無出世者，當知此住內異生品，非外異生。

Ru-Yi Translation

If, toward this very dharma, there is only the mundane, and no supramundane attainment, one should know that this abides in the class of the inner ordinary being, not the outsider ordinary being.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the inner ordinary being's own class (丑二內異生品), the second — a being who has attained the mundane five faculties within the inner dharma, but not yet any supramundane attainment, abides within the inner dharma's own preparatory stage — no longer an outsider, though not yet a noble one.

106. Section: Abiding Separately in the Remaining Class, Closing "The Abidings," "The Embrace of the Faculties," and the Entire Third Great Topic Entirely

If, toward this dharma, there is a supramundane attainment, one should know that all of this abides separately, in the remaining class, not belonging to those other classes.

Chinese Source

若於此法有出世者，當知一切別住餘品，非彼品類。

Ru-Yi Translation

If, toward this dharma, there is a supramundane attainment, one should know that all of this abides separately, in the remaining class, not belonging to those other classes.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the 披尋記

With this, abiding separately in the remaining class (丑三別住餘品) closes entirely, closing **the abidings (子四安住品類3: 外異生品/內異生品/別住餘品), the fourth and final of the embrace of the faculties' four sections, entirely** — a being who has actually attained a supramundane fruit abides in an entirely separate, noble class, transcending both the outsider and the inner ordinary being's own classes alike. The 披尋記 confirms this threefold division exhausts every possibility: the outsider ordinary being (lacking even mundane faculties), the inner ordinary being (mundane faculties only), and the noble class (supramundane attainment) — no fourth category remains. **With this section's closure, the embrace of the faculties (壬三根攝2: 安立/所行境/慧根最勝/安住品類), the third of the factors of enlightenment's nine great topics, closes in its complete entirety.**

107. Section: The Embrace of the Powers Begins — The Verse Marking and Listing

Further, a summary verse says: discernment, awakened wisdom and the rest; the nation and the rest, and the various kings; the arhat, the trainee; uprightness, coming last.

Chinese Source

復次，嗢柁南曰：

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, a summary verse says:

Chinese Source

思擇覺慧等　國等及諸王　阿羅漢有學　質直最為後

Ru-Yi Translation

Discernment, awakened wisdom and the rest; the nation and the rest, and the various kings; the arhat, the trainee; uprightness, coming last.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens **the embrace of the powers (壬四力攝2), the fourth of the factors of enlightenment's nine great topics**, named by the general summary verse's second line's third word "the powers" (神足及根力) — treating the five powers (信力/進力/念力/定力/慧力, sharing the same names as the five faculties but denoting their strengthened, unshakeable form) alongside a wider array of powers explored in this section — beginning with the verse marking and listing (癸一頌標列) — six matters unfold in the long-form explanation: discernment, awakened wisdom and the rest (four further powers), the nation and the various kings (a tenfold royal analogy), the arhat's own eight powers, the trainee's five powers (contrasted with the Tathāgata's own ten), and, finally, the upright practitioner's fourfold mark.

108. Section: The Long-Form Explanation Begins — Discernment Begins — Marking How the Name Is Attained

Briefly, toward every unwholesome deed belonging to the present dharma and to later dharmas, having deeply seen its fault, one is able to rightly discern, bringing every unwholesome deed to rest and cultivating every wholesome deed — this is called the power of discernment.

Chinese Source

略於一切現法、後法諸惡行中，深見過已，能正思擇，息諸惡行，修諸善行，名思擇力。

Ru-Yi Translation

Briefly, toward every unwholesome deed belonging to the present dharma and to later dharmas, having deeply seen its fault, one is able to rightly discern, bringing every unwholesome deed to rest and cultivating every wholesome deed — this is called the power of discernment.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens the long-form explanation (癸二長行釋6), a sixfold structure matching the verse's six matters, beginning with discernment (子一思擇2), named by the verse's first phrase's first half "discernment" (思擇覺慧等), beginning with marking how the name is attained (丑一標得名) — deeply seeing the fault in every unwholesome deed, present or future, and thereby bringing evil to rest while cultivating good, is what earns the name "power of discernment."

109. Section: Distinguishing Its Function Begins — Able to Accomplish Two Matters

One should know that this power is able to accomplish two matters: first, being able to go to a fortunate destiny among humans and gods; second, being able to go to nirvāṇa within the present dharma.

Chinese Source

當知此力能成二事。一者、能往人天善趣，二者、能往現法涅槃。

Ru-Yi Translation

One should know that this power is able to accomplish two matters: first, being able to go to a fortunate destiny among humans and gods; second, being able to go to nirvāṇa within the present dharma.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens distinguishing its function (丑二辨業用3), the second of discernment's two matters, beginning with able to accomplish two matters (寅一能成二事) — the power of discernment, understanding evil's own fault, leads either to a fortunate rebirth among gods and humans through cultivating the five precepts and ten wholesome deeds, or, through deeper reflection on selflessness, directly to nirvāṇa within this very life.

110. Section: Able to Rightly Cultivate

Further, this is able to serve as the support for the power of cultivation, which embraces the cultivation of the foundations of mindfulness; relying on this, one is able to rightly cultivate the four foundations of mindfulness and the other factors of enlightenment. One should know that this cultivation is called the power of cultivation.

Chinese Source

又此能與修習力攝修諸念住為所依止，由此為依，能正修習四念住等菩提分法。當知此修名修習力。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, this is able to serve as the support for the power of cultivation, which embraces the cultivation of the foundations of mindfulness; relying on this, one is able to rightly cultivate the four foundations of mindfulness and the other factors of enlightenment. One should know that this cultivation is called the power of cultivation.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Ground of Cultivation-Accomplished Wisdom

This opens able to rightly cultivate (寅二能正修習), the second — discernment serves as the very foundation for the power of cultivation, which, relying on it, rightly cultivates the four foundations of mindfulness and every other factor of enlightenment in turn. The Ground of Cultivation-Accomplished Wisdom describes this cultivation as resting on the preparatory stage of concentration, applying insight to the four noble truths, the contemplation of selflessness, and the three concentrations, or any observation conjoined with the noble path.

111. Section: Able to Accompany Shame Begins — Marking

Further, the power of discernment is able to serve as a companion to shame arising in three places.

Chinese Source

又思擇力，能與三處羞恥為伴。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, the power of discernment is able to serve as a companion to shame arising in three places.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens able to accompany shame (寅三能伴羞恥3), the third of distinguishing its function's three matters, beginning with marking (卯一標) — shame is the very first ornament of conduct; a practitioner with genuine shame is spurred to actively sever evil and cultivate good, and the power of discernment accompanies this shame in three distinct places.

112. Section: Questioning

What is called shame in three places?

Chinese Source

何等名為三處羞恥？

Ru-Yi Translation

What is called shame in three places?

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens questioning (卯二徵), the second of able to accompany shame's three matters.

113. Section: Explaining Begins — Distinguishing Each Mark Begins — Shame with Regard to Others

First, shame with regard to others. It means thinking thus: if I commit evil, I will be censured together by the buddhas and world-honored ones who possess the wisdom to know others' minds, by the noble disciples, and by the multitudes of gods who have faith in the Buddhadharma, throughout the world. This is called the power of discernment's first place.

Chinese Source

一者、他處羞恥。謂作是思：若我作惡，當為世間有他心智諸佛世尊、若聖弟子、若諸天眾信佛教者，共所呵毀。是名第一處思擇力。

Ru-Yi Translation

First, shame with regard to others. It means thinking thus: if I commit evil, I will be censured together by the buddhas and world-honored ones who possess the wisdom to know others' minds, by the noble disciples, and by the multitudes of gods who have faith in the Buddhadharma, throughout the world. This is called the power of discernment's first place.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens explaining (卯三釋2), the third of able to accompany shame's three matters, beginning with distinguishing each mark (辰一別辨相3), a threefold structure, beginning with shame with regard to others (巳一他處羞恥) — reflecting that any evil deed would be censured by every buddha, noble disciple, and faithful god who knows one's own mind gives rise to this first kind of shame; the wise, Master Cháng Bǎi notes, confess and repent the moment an evil thought is caught, never letting it take root and continue.

114. Section: Shame with Regard to Oneself

Second, shame with regard to oneself. It means thinking thus: if I commit evil, I will certainly be deeply censured by myself — what good person would commit such an evil deed? This is called the second place's increasing power.

Chinese Source

二者、自處羞恥。謂作是思：若我作惡，定當為己深所呵毀，何有善人為斯惡行。是名第二處增上力。

Ru-Yi Translation

Second, shame with regard to oneself. It means thinking thus: if I commit evil, I will certainly be deeply censured by myself — what good person would commit such an evil deed? This is called the second place's increasing power.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens shame with regard to oneself (巳二自處羞恥), the second — sustained self-reflection and self-respect, even before the noble path is attained, holds oneself accountable to one's own aspiration, censuring oneself whenever conduct falls short of it.

115. Section: Shame with Regard to the Dharma, Closing "Distinguishing Each Mark" Entirely

Third, shame with regard to the dharma. It means thinking thus: if I commit evil, I will thereby obstruct the holy life cultivated within the well-taught dharma and discipline; wherever this evil exists, it thereby destroys the holy life. This is called the power of discernment's third place.

Chinese Source

三者、法處羞恥。謂作是思：我若作惡，便為障礙於善說法毗奈耶中所修梵行；此法若有，便壞梵行。是名第三處思擇力。

Ru-Yi Translation

Third, shame with regard to the dharma. It means thinking thus: if I commit evil, I will thereby obstruct the holy life cultivated within the well-taught dharma and discipline; wherever this evil exists, it thereby destroys the holy life. This is called the power of discernment's third place.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

With this, shame with regard to the dharma (巳三法處羞恥) closes entirely, closing distinguishing each mark (辰一別辨相3: 他處/自處/法處羞恥) entirely — reflecting on dependent origination itself, an evil deed, however illusion-like, still bears its own illusion-like fruit, directly obstructing and destroying the very holy life one seeks to cultivate — a third, doctrinally-grounded source of shame.

116. Section: Concluding the Essential Meaning, Closing "Explaining," "Able to Accompany Shame," "Distinguishing Its Function," and "Discernment" Entirely

Such shame, one should know, has three places as its increase: first, the world's increase; second, one's own increase; third, the dharma's increase.

Chinese Source

如是羞恥，當知三處以為增上。一、世增上，二、自增上，三、法增上。

Ru-Yi Translation

Such shame, one should know, has three places as its increase: first, the world's increase; second, one's own increase; third, the dharma's increase.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing a substantial cluster of companion sūtras on discernment and cultivation as twin powers

With this, concluding the essential meaning (辰二結略義) closes entirely, closing explaining (卯三釋2: 別辨相/結略義) entirely, closing **able to accompany shame (寅三能伴羞恥3: 標/徵/釋) entirely**, closing **distinguishing its function (丑二辨業用3: 能成二事/能正修習/能伴羞恥) entirely**, closing **discernment (子一思擇2: 標得名/辨業用), the first of the embrace of the powers' six sections, entirely** — the world's, one's own, and the dharma's own increase together sustain the shame that discernment's power accompanies. Master Cháng Bǎi traces this section to a substantial cluster of companion sūtras teaching discernment (數力) and cultivation (修力) as a matched pair of powers — reflecting in a forest or beneath a tree that bodily, verbal, and mental evil conduct brings present and future retribution, self-reproach, censure by the wise, and eventual descent to the hells, thereby severing evil and cultivating good in turn; discernment maturing into cultivation, which perfects the four foundations of mindfulness and, ultimately, all nine dharmas (faith, diligence, mindfulness, concentration, wisdom, shame-with-regard-to-self, shame-with-regard-to-others, discernment, and cultivation) unpacked further in companion sūtras 54 and 55; and the four paths (preparatory, uninterrupted, liberation, and further advancement) and four dharma-phrases (well-spoken by the noble, spoken with love, spoken truthfully, spoken in accordance with the dharma) that discernment and cultivation together bring to completion.

117. Section: Awakened Wisdom and the Rest Begins — Marking and Listing

Further, through the increase of conduct that benefits oneself and conduct that benefits others, one should know that four powers are established: first, the power of awakened wisdom; second, the power of diligence; third, the power of blamelessness; fourth, the power of embracing others.

Chinese Source

復次，由自利行及利他行為增上故，當知建立有四種力。一、覺慧力，二、精進力，三、無罪力，四、攝受力。

Ru-Yi Translation

Further, through the increase of conduct that benefits oneself and conduct that benefits others, one should know that four powers are established: first, the power of awakened wisdom; second, the power of diligence; third, the power of blamelessness; fourth, the power of embracing others.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi

This opens awakened wisdom and the rest (子二覺慧等2), the second of the embrace of the powers' six sections, named by the verse's first phrase's second half "awakened wisdom and the rest" (思擇覺慧等), beginning with marking and listing (丑一標列) — four powers together strengthen both self-benefiting and other-benefiting conduct: the power of awakened wisdom (discerning, across six pairs, what is blameworthy or blameless, what should be cultivated or avoided, base or noble, dark or bright, discriminated or non-discriminated, and dependently-originated or not), the power of diligence (the same four right efforts underlying every other power), the power of blamelessness (sustained inward examination of body, speech, and mind, confessing and resolving never to repeat any fault found), and the power of embracing others (the four means of embrace — giving, kind speech, beneficial conduct, and cooperation — detailed further, per Master Cháng Bǎi, in the Bodhisattva Ground, fascicle 43, where these four are further unfolded into nine practices). Reading continues past this point to confirm what follows before further closure is claimed.

118. Section: Correlating and Explaining Begins — Toward One's Own Benefit Begins — Marking the Distinction

Being able to go to nirvāṇa within the present dharma is called one's own benefit; being able to go to a fortunate destiny among humans and gods is also called one's own benefit.

Chinese Source

能往現法涅槃，名為自義；能往人天善趣，亦名自義。

Ru-Yi Translation

Being able to go to nirvāṇa within the present dharma is called one's own benefit; being able to go to a fortunate destiny among humans and gods is also called one's own benefit.

Reading note — after Master Cháng Bǎi, citing the Bodhisattva Ground, fascicles 41-43

This opens correlating and explaining (丑二配釋2), the second of awakened wisdom and the rest's two matters, beginning with toward one's own benefit (寅一於自義2), beginning with marking the distinction (卯一標差別) — the first three of the four powers (awakened wisdom, diligence, blamelessness) all serve one's own benefit, which itself divides into two: present-life nirvāṇa (decisive excellence) and a fortunate rebirth among humans and gods (heightened existence).
